    17→## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
    18→
    19→This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution.
    20→
    21→### Core Principles
    22→
    23→- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is a self contained instruction file
    24→- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory
    25→- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order
    26→- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter
    27→- **Append-Only Building**: Build documents by appending content as directed

    29→### Step Processing Rules
    30→
    31→1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action

    33→3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: If a menu is presented, halt and wait for user selection
    34→4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue, only proceed when user selects 'C'
    35→5. **SAVE STATE**: Update frontmatter before loading next step
    36→6. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
    37→
    38→### Critical Rules
    39→
    40→- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
    41→- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
    42→- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence

     7→
     8→# Step 3: Extract
     9→
    10→## STEP GOAL:
    11→
    12→To extract all public exports, function signatures, type definitions, and co-import patterns from the source code using tier-appropriate tools, building a complete extraction inventory with confidence-tiered provenance citations.
    13→
    14→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    15→
    16→### Universal Rules:
    17→
    18→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    19→- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
    20→- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread

    22→

    24→
    25→- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine performing structural extraction
    26→- ✅ Zero hallucination tolerance — every extracted item must trace to source code
    27→- ✅ Confidence tier labeling is mandatory for every extraction result
    28→
    29→### Step-Specific Rules:
    30→
    31→- 🎯 Focus ONLY on extracting exports, signatures, types from source code
    32→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to compile SKILL.md content — that's step-05
    33→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to write any output files — extraction stays in context
    34→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to include content that cannot be cited to a source location
    35→- ⚒️ Every extracted item MUST have a provenance citation: `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` or `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`
    36→
    37→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    38→
    39→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    40→- 💾 Build extraction inventory in context — do not write files
    41→- 📖 Load extraction patterns data file for tier-specific strategy
    42→- 🚫 If an export cannot be verified, exclude it — do not guess
    43→
    44→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    45→
    46→- Available: brief_data, tier, source_location, file_tree from step-01; ecosystem_status from step-02
    47→- Focus: Source code extraction and inventory building
    48→- Limits: Do NOT compile, assemble, or write any output
    49→- Dependencies: Source code must be accessible (resolved in step-01)
    50→
    51→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    54→
    55→### 1. Load Extraction Patterns
    56→
    57→Load `{extractionPatternsData}` completely. Identify the strategy for the current forge tier.
    58→
    59→### 2. Apply Scope Filters
    60→
    61→From the brief, apply scope and pattern filters:
    62→
    63→- `scope` — determines what to extract (e.g., "all public exports", specific modules)
    64→- `include_patterns` — file globs to include (if specified)
    65→- `exclude_patterns` — file globs to exclude (if specified)
    66→
    67→Build the filtered file list from the source tree resolved in step-01.
    68→
    69→### 3. Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction
    70→
    71→**Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only):**
    72→
    73→If `source_repo` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
    74→
    75→If `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
    76→
    77→1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
    78→
    79→2. **Ephemeral shallow clone:** Clone the repository to a system temp path for AST access:
    80→
    81→   ```
    82→   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
    83→   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
    84→   ```
    85→
    86→   If `include_patterns` are specified in the brief, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:
    87→
    88→   ```
    89→   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}
    90→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {include_patterns}
    91→   ```
    92→
    93→3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Proceed with the **Forge/Deep Tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
    94→
    95→4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**
    96→
    97→   ⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:**
    98→
    99→   "Ephemeral clone of `{source_repo}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path."
   100→
   101→   Proceed with Quick tier extraction strategy below. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
   102→
   103→**Ephemeral clone cleanup:** After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting the Gate 2 summary (Section 5), if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete the `{temp_path}` directory. Log: "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up." This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing.
   104→
   105→**Quick Tier (No AST tools):**
   106→
   107→1. Use `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` to map source structure (if remote)
   108→2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
   109→3. Use `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` to read each entry point
   110→4. Extract from source text: exported function names, parameter lists, return types
   111→5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations
   112→6. Confidence: All results T1-low — `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`
   113→
   114→**Forge/Deep Tier (AST available):**
   115→
   116→1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
   117→2. Use `ast_bridge.scan_definitions(path, language)` for each file in scope
   118→3. For each export: extract function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
   119→4. Use `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports(path, libraries[])` to find integration points
   120→5. Build extraction rules YAML data for reproducibility
   121→6. Confidence: All results T1 — `[AST:{file}:L{line}]`
   122→
   123→**If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier:**
   124→
   125→⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:** "AST tools are unavailable — extraction will use source reading (T1-low). Run [SF] Setup Forge to detect and configure AST tools for T1 confidence."
   126→
   127→Degrade to Quick tier extraction. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
   128→
   129→**For each file — handle failures gracefully:**
   130→
   131→- If a file cannot be read: log warning, skip file, continue with remaining files
   132→- If AST parsing fails on a file: fall back to source reading for that file, continue
   133→
   134→### 4. Build Extraction Inventory
   135→
   136→Compile all extracted data into a structured inventory:
   137→
   138→**Per-export entry:**
   139→- Function/type name
   140→- Full signature with types
   141→- Parameters (name, type, required/optional)
   142→- Return type
   143→- Source file and line number
   144→- Provenance citation (`[AST:...]` or `[SRC:...]`)
   145→- Confidence tier (T1 or T1-low)
   146→
   147→**Aggregate counts:**
   148→- Total files scanned
   149→- Total exports found
   150→- Exports by type (functions, types/interfaces, constants)
   151→- Confidence breakdown (T1 count, T1-low count)
   152→
   153→**Co-import patterns (Forge/Deep only):**
   154→- Libraries commonly imported alongside extracted exports
   155→- Integration point suggestions
   156→
   157→### 5. Present Extraction Summary (Gate 2)
   158→
   159→Display the extraction findings for user confirmation:
   160→
   161→"**Extraction complete.**
   162→
   163→**Files scanned:** {file_count}
   164→**Exports found:** {export_count} ({function_count} functions, {type_count} types, {constant_count} constants)
   165→**Confidence:** {t1_count} T1 (AST-verified), {t1_low_count} T1-low (source reading)
   166→**Tier used:** {tier}
   167→**Co-import patterns:** {pattern_count} detected
   168→
   169→**Top exports:**
   170→{list top 10 exports with signatures}
   171→
   172→{warnings if any files skipped or degraded}
   173→
   174→Review the extraction summary above. Select an option to continue."
   175→
   176→### 6. Present MENU OPTIONS
   177→
   178→Display: "**Extraction Summary — Select an Option:** [C] Continue to compilation"
   179→
   180→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   181→
   182→- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting the extraction summary
   183→- This is Gate 2 — user must confirm before compilation proceeds
   184→- User may ask questions about the extraction results before continuing
   185→
   186→#### Menu Handling Logic:
   187→
   188→- IF C: Confirm extraction inventory is complete. Immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`
   189→- IF Any other comments or queries: answer questions about the extraction results, then redisplay the menu
   190→
   191→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   192→
   193→ONLY WHEN the extraction inventory is built with provenance citations and the user has confirmed the extraction summary will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for temporal context fetching.
   194→
   195→---
   196→
   197→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   198→
   199→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   200→
   201→- Extraction patterns loaded and tier-appropriate strategy applied
   202→- Scope filters applied from brief
   203→- All accessible source files in scope scanned
   204→- Every extracted item has a provenance citation with confidence tier
   205→- Extraction inventory built with aggregate counts
   206→- Graceful degradation if AST tools unavailable
   207→- Gate 2 summary presented with export counts and confidence breakdown
   208→- User confirmed before proceeding

   211→
   212→- Including exports without provenance citations

   219→
   220→**Master Rule:** Zero hallucination — every extraction must trace to source code. Uncitable content is excluded, not guessed.

     1→---
     2→name: 'step-04-enrich'
     3→description: 'QMD knowledge enrichment for extracted functions — Deep tier only, skip for Quick/Forge'
     4→nextStepFile: './step-05-compile.md'
     5→---
     6→
     7→# Step 4: Enrich
     8→
     9→## STEP GOAL:
    10→
    11→To enrich the extraction inventory with temporal context from QMD knowledge searches — issues, PRs, changelogs, and migration notes that add T2-confidence annotations to extracted functions. Deep tier only; Quick and Forge tiers skip this step entirely.
    12→
    13→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    14→
    15→### Universal Rules:
    16→
    17→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    18→- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
    19→- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
    20→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    21→
    22→### Role Reinforcement:
    23→
    24→- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine performing knowledge enrichment
    25→- ✅ QMD enrichment adds temporal depth — it never replaces AST extraction
    26→- ✅ Enrichment failure is graceful degradation, not an error
    27→
    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- 🎯 Focus ONLY on QMD searches to annotate extracted functions
    31→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to modify extraction results — enrichment is additive only
    32→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to begin compilation — that's step-05
    33→- 💬 QMD failures degrade gracefully — continue without enrichment
    34→- ⏱️ Quick and Forge tiers: skip this step entirely, auto-proceed
    35→
    36→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    37→
    38→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    39→- 💾 Enrichment annotations are added to extraction inventory in context
    40→- 📖 QMD results become T2-confidence annotations
    41→- 🚫 Never replace T1 extraction data with QMD findings
    42→
    43→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    44→
    45→- Available: extraction_inventory from step-03, brief_data, tier from step-01
    46→- Focus: Adding temporal context to extracted functions
    47→- Limits: Do NOT modify extraction results, only annotate them
    48→- Dependencies: Extraction inventory must exist from step-03
    49→
    50→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    51→
    52→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    53→
    54→### 1. Check Tier Eligibility
    55→
    56→**If tier is Quick or Forge:**
    57→
    58→Auto-proceed silently. Display no message. Immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
    59→
    60→**If tier is Deep:**
    61→
    62→Continue to step 2.
    63→
    64→### 2. Collection Inventory Pre-check (Deep Tier Only)
    65→
    66→Before searching, check which QMD collections are available:
    67→
    68→1. Read the sidecar `forge-tier.yaml` to get registered `qmd_collections` entries
    69→2. Identify which collections contain temporal context — collections with `type` other than `"brief"` (e.g., issues, PRs, changelogs, architecture notes)
    70→3. **If no temporal collections exist** (only `"brief"` type or no collections at all): report this and auto-proceed. Display:
    71→
    72→"**Enrichment: no temporal collections available.**
    73→Only brief-type QMD collections found — no issues, PRs, or changelogs indexed. Enrichment skipped (expected for first-run skill creation). T2 enrichment becomes available when temporal context is indexed into QMD collections.
    74→
    75→Proceeding to compilation..."
    76→
    77→Then immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
    78→
    79→4. **If temporal collections exist:** Continue to step 3.
    80→
    81→### 3. QMD Enrichment Searches (Deep Tier Only)
    82→
    83→For each major exported function — limited to the **top-level public API surface** (typically 10-20 functions that will appear in the context-snippet), not all extracted exports — search the temporal QMD collections for context:
    84→
    85→**Search targets per function:**
    86→
    87→1. **Issues/PRs:** `qmd_bridge.search("{function_name}")` — find related discussions, bug reports, feature requests
    88→2. **Changelog entries:** `qmd_bridge.search("{function_name} changelog")` — find version history, breaking changes
    89→3. **Migration notes:** `qmd_bridge.vector_search("{function_name} migration deprecated")` — find deprecation or migration context
    90→
    91→**For each QMD result:**
    92→
    93→- Create a T2 annotation: `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]`
    94→- Classify temporal relevance:
    95→  - **T2-past:** Historical context (why it was built, what it replaced)
    96→  - **T2-future:** Forward-looking context (planned changes, deprecation warnings)
    97→
    98→**Handling QMD failures:**
    99→
   100→- If qmd_bridge is unavailable: skip all enrichment, note in context
   101→- If individual search fails: skip that function's enrichment, continue with others
   102→- If QMD returns no results for a function: no annotation added (absence is normal)
   103→
   104→### 4. Annotate Extraction Inventory
   105→
   106→Add enrichment annotations to the extraction inventory without modifying extraction data:
   107→

> AGENT

Good question. Here's how AST extraction and QMD work together in the Deep tier pipeline:

**They are two separate, complementary layers — AST does NOT feed into QMD.**

| Step | Source | Produces | Confidence |
|------|--------|----------|------------|
| **Step-03 Extract** | Source code via AST tools | Structural inventory (exports, signatures, types) | **T1** — `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` |
| **Step-03b Fetch Temporal** | GitHub API via `gh` CLI (issues, PRs, releases, changelog) | QMD temporal collection | Raw temporal data |
| **Step-04 Enrich** | Searches QMD collections using function names from step-03 | T2 annotations added to the extraction inventory | **T2** — `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]` |

**The flow:**
1. **AST extraction** (step-03) builds a structural inventory of what the code exports — this is ground truth from source.
2. **Temporal fetching** (step-03b) separately populates QMD with historical context (issues, PRs, changelogs) from GitHub — no AST data goes into QMD.
3. **Enrichment** (step-04) then searches QMD using the top-level public API function names (~10-20) from the extraction inventory, annotating them with T2 context (e.g., "this function had a breaking change in v2.0", "this is being deprecated per issue #42").

**Key rules:**
- Enrichment is **additive only** — T1 extraction data is never modified by QMD results
- QMD contains **temporal/historical** data, not structural/code data
- The AST inventory drives **what to search for** in QMD, but doesn't populate QMD itself

---

Now, back to the edit workflow — **what would you like to edit?**

- **[B]rief** — Module brief
- **[Y]aml** — module.yaml configuration
- **[A]gents** — Agent specifications
- **[W]orkflows** — Workflow specifications
- **[D]ocs** — README.md or TODO.md

> AGENT

I see several improvement opportunities after reading the full pipeline. Here's my analysis:

---

## Findings

### 1. False Dependency — Step-03b doesn't use extraction results

Step-03b declares `extraction_inventory from step-03` in its context boundaries, but **never actually uses it**. The temporal fetches are entirely generic:

```bash
gh issue list -R {owner}/{repo} --limit 100  # generic, no function filtering
gh pr list -R {owner}/{repo} --limit 100      # same
```

This means you fetch 100 random issues/PRs for a repo that might have thousands — most irrelevant to the extracted API surface.

### 2. Missed Opportunity — Targeted temporal fetching

Since step-03 runs before 03b, the extraction inventory **is available**. Step-03b could use extracted function names to do **targeted fetches**:

```bash
gh search issues --repo {owner}/{repo} "{function_name}" --limit 20
```

This would produce a much higher-signal QMD collection, making step-04 enrichment dramatically more effective.

### 3. Step-04 search queries are shallow

Current enrichment searches:
- `search("{function_name}")` — bare keyword
- `search("{function_name} changelog")` — keyword + "changelog"
- `vector_search("{function_name} migration deprecated")` — single semantic query

These miss context. If extraction knows `function_name` lives in module `graph.neo4j`, a search for `"neo4j graph function_name"` would surface far more relevant issues. The extraction inventory has module/file context that enrichment ignores.

### 4. No enrichment-to-compilation feedback for deprecation/migration

If step-04 discovers "function X was renamed to Y in v3" from QMD, that insight stays as a passive T2 annotation. Step-05 compile doesn't have instructions to surface these as **migration warnings** or **deprecation notes** in SKILL.md. The data is collected but underused.

---

## Proposed Improvements

| # | Change | Where | Impact |
|---|--------|-------|--------|
| **A** | Make step-03b use extraction results for targeted `gh search` queries instead of generic list fetches | `step-03b-fetch-temporal.md` | Higher-signal temporal collection |
| **B** | Pass module/file context from extraction into step-04 QMD searches | `step-04-enrich.md` | More relevant T2 annotations |
| **C** | Add migration/deprecation surfacing instructions to step-05 compile | `step-05-compile.md` | T2 data actually reaches the user in SKILL.md |
| **D** | Fix the false context boundary declaration in step-03b (either use it or remove it) | `step-03b-fetch-temporal.md` | Accuracy of documentation |

**A + B + C** form a coherent improvement chain: better data in → better search → better output. **D** is a cleanup either way.

Want me to proceed with implementing these? All of them, or a subset?

    52→- **Secondary Agent**: Complementary perspective or alternative approach
    53→- **Tertiary Agent**: Cross-domain insight or devil's advocate (if beneficial)
    54→
    55→**Priority Rules:**

    97→### 5. Question Handling Protocol
    98→

🎉 **PARTY MODE ACTIVATED!** 🎉

Welcome Armel! The team is assembled for a deep impact analysis of the proposed Deep tier pipeline improvements. I've selected the agents best suited to dissect this without missing anything:

- 🏗️ **Winston** (Architect) — pipeline architecture, dependency analysis, data flow trade-offs
- 🔄 **Wendy** (Workflow Builder) — step-file integrity, sequencing impacts, state propagation
- 🏗️ **Morgan** (Module Builder) — module-level coherence, cross-workflow impact, integration patterns

Let's get into it. The topic: **4 proposed changes to the Deep tier create-skill pipeline (steps 03, 03b, 04, 05) — find breaking changes, missed impacts, and gaps.**

---

🏗️ **Winston**: Right. Before we touch a single file, let's map what we're actually dealing with. The pipeline has a **data dependency chain** that flows through context — not files — which makes it fragile to sequence changes. Let me trace it:

```
step-01 → brief_data, tier, source_location
step-02 → ecosystem_status
step-03 → extraction_inventory (T1)     ← structural ground truth
step-03b → QMD temporal collection       ← side-effect: writes to QMD + forge-tier.yaml
step-04 → enrichment_annotations (T2)   ← reads QMD, annotates inventory
step-05 → compiled artifacts             ← consumes inventory + annotations
step-06 → validation results
step-07 → writes files + registers extraction QMD collection
```

The proposed change **A** (targeted temporal fetching) would create a **real dependency** from 03b on 03's extraction inventory. Currently 03b falsely declares this dependency but doesn't use it — making it real is the honest thing to do, but it closes the door on future parallelization. That's a one-way architectural decision. Are we comfortable with that?

---

🔄 **Wendy**: Winston's right to flag that, but let me check the actual step-file contracts. I need to verify what the `CONTEXT BOUNDARIES` sections declare.

Step-03b currently says:
> `Available: brief_data, tier, source_location from step-01; extraction_inventory from step-03`

So the **contract already declares the dependency**. The problem is the implementation doesn't honor it. If we make 03b actually consume extraction results, we're *fulfilling* the existing contract, not changing it. That's not a breaking change — it's a bug fix.

**However** — here's what worries me: step-03b's `gh` CLI fetches are **slow** (100 issues + 100 PRs + releases + changelog). If we switch to targeted `gh search` per-function, we go from ~4 API calls to potentially **10-20 calls** (one per top-level export). That's a latency multiplier. If rate limiting kicks in on `gh`, the entire step becomes unreliable.

The current generic fetch is a blunt instrument, but it's **predictable and fast**. Targeted fetching is smarter but riskier.

---

🏗️ **Morgan**: Both valid points. Let me zoom out to the module level. The `create-skill` workflow isn't the only consumer of these QMD collections. Let me check what else reads `forge-tier.yaml` and `{name}-temporal` collections.

Looking at step-07, it registers a `{name}-extraction` collection. The `test-skill` workflow has step-04b (`external-validators`) and step-05 (`score`) which reference forge-tier data. The `audit-skill` and `update-skill` workflows (if they exist) would also consume these collections.

**The real question is: if we change what goes INTO the temporal collection (targeted vs generic), do we break any downstream consumers?**

Step-04 searches the temporal collection by function name. If the collection only contains issues that mention those function names (because 03b fetched them targeted), step-04 will find the same results — it's just that the collection is smaller and more focused. That's **not a breaking change** for step-04.

But what about `update-skill`? If a future re-run of create-skill produces a differently-scoped temporal collection because the extraction inventory changed (new exports discovered), the QMD collection content would shift. The 7-day cache in step-03b means stale targeted fetches could persist even when extraction results change. **That's a subtle coherence bug.**

---

🏗️ **Winston**: Morgan just found something important. Let me dig into this cache coherence issue.

Currently:
```yaml
# forge-tier.yaml
- name: "cognee-temporal"
  type: "temporal"
  created_at: "2026-03-10"  # cached for 7 days
```

If we re-run create-skill with different scope filters (different `include_patterns`), step-03 produces a **different extraction inventory**, but step-03b sees the cached collection is fresh and **skips the re-fetch**. Now step-04 searches a temporal collection that was targeted at the OLD extraction results.

**Proposed fix for change A:** The cache check in step-03b section 2 should hash or fingerprint the extraction inventory's top-level function list. If the function list changed since the cached collection was built, invalidate the cache regardless of age.

---

🔄 **Wendy**: Good catch. Now let me examine **change B** (passing module/file context into step-04 searches). The current step-04 searches are:

```
search("{function_name}")
search("{function_name} changelog")
vector_search("{function_name} migration deprecated")
```

If we add module context like `search("neo4j graph {function_name}")`, we improve relevance. But step-04 says it's scoped to the **top-level public API surface (10-20 functions)**. Each function already gets 3 searches. Adding module context means we might go to 4-5 searches per function. That's 60-100 QMD queries for a single enrichment pass.

Is QMD fast enough for that? The MCP instructions say `search` is ~30ms and `vector_search` is ~2s. So:
- Current: 20 functions × (2 keyword + 1 vector) = 40 keyword (~1.2s) + 20 vector (~40s) = **~41s**
- Proposed: 20 functions × (3 keyword + 2 vector) = 60 keyword (~1.8s) + 40 vector (~80s) = **~82s**

That doubles enrichment time. For a step that's supposed to be auto-proceed, that's a noticeable wall-clock increase.

**My recommendation:** Don't add more search queries. Instead, make the existing queries **smarter** by prepending the module path to the function name: `search("{module}/{function_name}")`. Same query count, better context. One change, not a multiplication.

---

🏗️ **Morgan**: Wendy's latency analysis is sharp. Now let me look at **change C** — surfacing migration/deprecation from T2 annotations in step-05 compile.

Step-05 section 9 (Tier 2 — Full API Reference) already says:
> `T2 annotations (Deep tier only): temporal context from enrichment`

So there's already an instruction to include T2 data. But it's buried in the reference-eligible Tier 2 section. If split-body extracts Tier 2 into `references/`, the migration warnings disappear from SKILL.md.

**The real gap is Tier 1.** There's no instruction in sections 1-8 to surface critical T2 findings inline. A deprecation warning is arguably more important than an architecture overview, yet it would only appear in the split-out reference files.

I'd propose adding a **Section 4b — Migration & Deprecation Warnings** to Tier 1 (always inline). Rules:
- Only populated if step-04 found T2-future annotations (deprecation, rename, breaking change)
- Max 10 lines — just the warnings, not full context
- Links to the Tier 2 reference for details
- Empty/skipped if no warnings found

This keeps SKILL.md actionable even after split-body.

---

🏗️ **Winston**: Morgan's proposal for a Tier 1 migration section is architecturally sound. But it introduces a new question: **how does step-05 know which T2 annotations are "critical" enough for Tier 1?**

Currently T2 annotations are classified as:
- `T2-past` — historical context (why it was built)
- `T2-future` — forward-looking (planned changes, deprecation)

The rule is simple: `T2-future` goes to Tier 1 warnings, `T2-past` stays in Tier 2 reference. No new classification needed — the data model already supports this.

**But here's an impact I want to flag for change D** (fixing the false context boundary). Step-03b currently says:

> `Available: brief_data, tier, source_location from step-01; extraction_inventory from step-03`

If we change this to actually use `extraction_inventory`, we need to verify that the extraction inventory **format** is stable. Step-03 builds it in-context, not as a file. If someone later changes the extraction inventory structure in step-03, they'd now silently break step-03b too. That's a **coupling risk**.

**Recommendation:** Don't pass the full inventory. Step-03b only needs the **list of top-level function names** for targeted searching. Define a minimal contract: step-03b consumes `extraction_inventory.top_exports[]` — a flat list of function names. This limits the coupling surface.

---

🔄 **Wendy**: Winston's minimal-contract idea is exactly right. Let me also flag **one more thing everyone's missed**: the `test-skill` workflow.

Step-04b-external-validators and step-05-score in the test workflow consume the generated SKILL.md and score it. If we add a new Tier 1 section (Migration & Deprecation Warnings), the **scoring rubric in `scoring-rules.md` needs updating**. Otherwise a SKILL.md with this section gets scored against criteria that don't expect it — it could either be ignored (harmless) or cause a structural mismatch if the scorer validates section ordering.

Let me also check: does step-06-validate in create-skill validate section structure? If so, the validation rules need updating too.

**Cross-workflow impact summary:**
1. `create-skill/step-05` — add Tier 1 migration section instructions
2. `create-skill/step-06` — update validation to expect optional migration section  
3. `create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` — add migration section to the section structure data
4. `test-skill/data/scoring-rules.md` — update rubric for new section
5. `test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md` — may need awareness of new section

---

🏗️ **Morgan**: Wendy just expanded the blast radius significantly. Let me consolidate everything into a single impact map:

**Change A — Targeted temporal fetching (step-03b):**
- Files: `step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`
- New dependency: consumes `extraction_inventory.top_exports[]` from step-03
- Cache invalidation: needs extraction fingerprint check, not just age
- Risk: more API calls, potential `gh` rate limiting
- Mitigation: use `gh search` with batch queries, cap at 5-10 targeted searches

**Change B — Smarter enrichment queries (step-04):**
- Files: `step-04-enrich.md`
- Approach: prefix module path to existing queries, don't add new queries
- Risk: minimal — same query count, better relevance
- No downstream impact

**Change C — Tier 1 migration section (step-05):**
- Files: `step-05-compile.md`, `data/skill-sections.md`
- Cross-workflow: `step-06-validate.md`, `test-skill/data/scoring-rules.md`
- Risk: new optional section must be handled as absent for Quick/Forge tiers
- The `update-skill` workflow (if/when built) must preserve this section

**Change D — Fix false context boundary (step-03b):**
- Files: `step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`
- Actually absorbed into Change A — if we implement A, D is resolved
- Minimal contract: only consume function name list, not full inventory

---

🏗️ **Winston**: One final architectural concern. All four changes are **Deep tier only**. Quick and Forge tiers are unaffected — they skip step-03b and step-04 entirely. That limits the blast radius significantly.

But there's a subtle implication: if change C adds a "Migration & Deprecation Warnings" section to SKILL.md, and this section only gets populated for Deep tier (because only Deep tier has T2 annotations), then **the SKILL.md structure differs by tier**. Quick/Forge skills won't have this section. That's fine if the section is truly optional, but the `split-body` tool and any parsers need to handle its absence gracefully.

**My recommendation for execution order:**
1. Start with **B** (safest — just improve existing query strings in step-04)
2. Then **A + D** together (make 03b use extraction results + fix contract)
3. Then **C** last (most cross-cutting — touches compile, validate, test-skill scoring)

This way each change builds on confirmed stability from the previous one.

---

Armel, the team has mapped out the full impact surface. The key findings:

| Finding | Severity | Agents |
|---------|----------|--------|
| Cache coherence bug if extraction scope changes between runs | High | Winston, Morgan |
| Latency doubling risk if too many targeted queries added | Medium | Wendy |
| Missing Tier 1 surface for T2-future deprecation warnings | Medium | Morgan |
| Cross-workflow scoring rubric needs update for new section | Medium | Wendy |
| Coupling risk if full extraction inventory is passed to 03b | Low | Winston |
| SKILL.md structural variance by tier for optional sections | Low | Winston |

Feel free to continue the discussion with the team, address specific agents, or ask deeper questions.

     1→# SKILL.md Section Structure
     2→
     3→## agentskills.io Compliant Format
     4→
     5→### Frontmatter (Required — agentskills.io Compliance)
     6→
     7→```yaml
     8→---
     9→name: {skill-name}
    10→description: >
    11→  {Trigger-optimized description of what the skill does and when to use it.
    12→  Include specific keywords for agent discovery.
    13→  Mention what NOT to use it for if applicable.
    14→  1-1024 characters.}
    15→---
    16→```
    17→
    18→**Frontmatter rules (agentskills.io specification):**
    19→
    20→- `name`: 1-64 characters, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens only, must match parent directory name
    21→- `description`: 1-1024 characters, trigger-optimized for agent matching
    22→- Only 6 fields permitted: `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools`
    23→- `version` and `author` belong in metadata.json, NOT in frontmatter
    24→
    25→### Two-Tier Section Structure
    26→
    27→SKILL.md uses a two-tier structure to ensure actionable content survives `split-body` extraction.
    28→
    29→#### Tier 1 — Always Inline (survives split-body, target <300 lines)
    30→
    31→| # | Section | Budget | Purpose |
    32→|---|---------|--------|---------|
    33→| 1 | **Overview** | ~10 lines | What the library does, source repo, version, tier, export count |
    34→| 2 | **Quick Start** | ~30 lines | 3-5 core functions with one runnable end-to-end example |
    35→| 3 | **Common Workflows** | ~30 lines | 4-5 typical function call sequences for common tasks |
    36→| 4 | **Key API Summary** | ~20 lines | Table of top 10-15 functions (name, purpose, key params) |
    37→| 5 | **Key Types** | ~20 lines | Most important enum/type values inline |
    38→| 6 | **Architecture at a Glance** | ~10 lines | Bullet list of subsystem categories |
    39→| 7 | **CLI** | ~10 lines | Basic CLI commands (skip if no CLI) |
    40→| 8 | **Manual Sections** | ~5 lines | `<!-- [MANUAL] -->` markers for update-skill |
    41→
    42→#### Tier 2 — Reference-Eligible (extracted by split-body into references/)
    43→
    44→| # | Section | Purpose |
    45→|---|---------|---------|
    46→| 9 | **Full API Reference** | Complete signatures, parameter tables, return types, examples, citations |
    47→| 10 | **Full Type Definitions** | All types, interfaces, enums with full field details |
    48→| 11 | **Full Integration Patterns** | Co-import patterns, adapter details, pipeline internals (Forge/Deep only) |
    49→
    50→#### Assembly Rules
    51→
    52→- Tier 1 sections are assembled first — they form the standalone body
    53→- Tier 2 sections are assembled after — they are progressive disclosure detail
    54→- Tier 1 sections are kept short enough that `split-body` targets the larger Tier 2 sections (`## Full` headings) into `references/`
    55→- After split-body, SKILL.md retains all Tier 1 content — actionable without loading references
    56→- An agent loading only SKILL.md (no references) must get enough to act
    57→
    58→### Provenance Citation Format
    59→
    60→| Tier | Format | Example |
    61→|------|--------|---------|
    62→| T1 (AST) | `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` | `[AST:src/auth/index.ts:L42]` |
    63→| T1-low (Source) | `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]` | `[SRC:src/auth/index.ts:L42]` |
    64→| T2 (QMD) | `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]` | `[QMD:project:CHANGELOG.md]` |
    65→| T3 (External) | `[EXT:{url}]` | `[EXT:docs.example.com/api]` |
    66→
    67→### [MANUAL] Section Markers
    68→
    69→Seed empty manual sections for future update-skill compatibility:
    70→
    71→```markdown
    72→<!-- [MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
    73→<!-- Add custom notes here. This section is preserved during skill updates. -->
    74→<!-- [/MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
    75→```
    76→
    77→Place after Quick Start and after API Reference sections.
    78→
    79→---
    80→
    81→## context-snippet.md Format
    82→
    83→Compressed 2-line-per-skill format for CLAUDE.md managed section:
    84→
    85→```markdown
    86→{skill-name} -> skills/{skill-name}/
    87→  exports: {comma-separated top 10 function names}
    88→```
    89→
    90→---
    91→
    92→## metadata.json Structure
    93→
    94→```json
    95→{
    96→  "name": "{skill-name}",
    97→  "version": "{source-version}",
    98→  "skill_type": "single",
    99→  "source_authority": "{official|community|internal}",
   100→  "source_repo": "{github-url}",
   101→  "source_commit": "{commit-hash}",
   102→  "confidence_tier": "{Quick|Forge|Deep}",
   103→  "spec_version": "1.3",
   104→  "generation_date": "{ISO-8601}",
   105→  "exports": [],
   106→  "tool_versions": {
   107→    "ast_grep": "{version-or-null}",
   108→    "qmd": "{version-or-null}",
   109→    "skf": "1.0.0"
   110→  },
   111→  "stats": {
   112→    "exports_documented": 0,
   113→    "exports_total": 0,
   114→    "coverage": 0.0,
   115→    "confidence_t1": 0,
   116→    "confidence_t2": 0,
   117→    "confidence_t3": 0
   118→  },
   119→  "dependencies": [],
   120→  "compatibility": "{semver-range}"
   121→}
   122→```
   123→
   124→---
   125→
   126→## references/ Directory Structure
   127→
   128→One file per major function group or type:
   129→
   130→```
   131→references/
   132→├── {function-group-a}.md    — Detailed reference with full examples
   133→├── {function-group-b}.md    — Detailed reference with full examples
   134→└── {type-name}.md           — Type definition details
   135→```
   136→
   137→Each reference file includes:
   138→- Full function signatures
   139→- Detailed parameter descriptions
   140→- Return value details
   141→- Complete usage examples
   142→- Related functions cross-references
   143→- Temporal annotations (Deep tier: T2-past, T2-future)
   144→
   145→---
   146→
   147→## provenance-map.json Structure
   148→
   149→```json
   150→{
   151→  "skill_name": "{name}",
   152→  "source_repo": "{url}",
   153→  "source_commit": "{hash}",
   154→  "generated_at": "{ISO-8601}",

   167→
   168→---

   170→## evidence-report.md Structure

     6→
     7→# Step 6: Validate
     8→
     9→## STEP GOAL:
    10→
    11→To validate the compiled SKILL.md content against the agentskills.io specification using skill-check, auto-fix any validation failures, and confirm spec compliance before artifact generation.
    12→
    13→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    14→
    15→### Universal Rules:
    16→
    17→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    18→- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
    19→- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
    20→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`

    23→
    24→- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine performing quality assurance
    25→- ✅ Validation ensures spec compliance — it does not modify content semantics
    26→- ✅ Tool unavailability means skip validation, not halt workflow
    27→
    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- 🎯 Focus ONLY on validating compiled content against spec
    31→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to add new content — only fix spec compliance issues
    32→- 💾 Validation and auto-fix modify files in the staging directory (`_bmad-output/{name}/`)
    33→- 💬 If auto-fix fails, report issues clearly but proceed (warn, don't halt)
    34→- ⚙️ If skill-check unavailable: skip validation, add warning to evidence report
    35→- ⚠️ Ignore non-zero exit codes from `skill-check` if the JSON output shows 0 errors — parse JSON output, not exit codes
    36→

    39→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    40→- 💾 Validation results are added to evidence-report content in context
    41→- 📖 Auto-fix pattern: validate → fix → re-validate (once)
    42→- 🚫 Maximum one auto-fix attempt per validation failure
    43→
    44→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    45→
    46→- Available: All compiled content from step-05 (SKILL.md, metadata.json, etc.)
    47→- Focus: Spec compliance validation and auto-fix
    48→- Limits: Do NOT add new content or modify extraction data
    49→- Dependencies: Compiled content must exist from step-05
    50→
    51→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    54→
    55→### 1. Check Tool Availability
    56→
    57→Run: `npx skill-check -h`
    58→
    59→- If succeeds (returns usage information): Continue to automated validation (section 2)
    60→- If fails (command not found or error): Perform manual fallback (section 3)
    61→  - Add note to evidence-report content: "Spec validation performed manually — skill-check tool unavailable"
    62→
    63→**Important:** Use the verification command. Do not assume availability — empirical check required.
    64→
    65→### 2. Validate & Auto-Fix (skill-check check --fix)
    66→
    67→Run the external skill-check tool against the compiled skill staging directory:
    68→
    69→```bash
    70→npx skill-check check <staging-skill-dir> --fix --format json --no-security-scan
    71→```
    72→
    73→This single command performs:
    74→- Frontmatter validation (required fields, naming, ordering, unknown fields)
    75→- Description quality checks (length, "Use when" phrasing)
    76→- Body limit enforcement (line count, token count)
    77→- Local link resolution
    78→- File formatting (trailing newlines)

   132→Then re-validate to confirm the fix:
   133→
   134→```bash
   135→npx skill-check check <staging-skill-dir> --format json --no-security-scan
   136→```
   137→
   138→**If skill-check unavailable or no body size issue:** Skip this step.
   139→
   140→### 5. Security Scan
   141→
   142→**If skill-check is available**, run security scan on the compiled skill:
   143→
   144→```bash
   145→npx skill-check check <staging-skill-dir> --format json
   146→```
   147→
   148→(Security scan is enabled by default when `--no-security-scan` is omitted.)
   149→
   150→**Parse security findings** from the JSON output:
   151→- Record any security warnings (prompt injection risks, unsafe patterns) in evidence-report
   152→- Security findings are advisory — they do not block artifact generation
   153→
   154→**If skill-check unavailable:** Skip with note: "Security scan skipped — skill-check tool unavailable"
   155→
   156→### 6. Content Quality Review (tessl)
   157→
   158→**If tessl is available**, run a content quality review on the compiled skill:
   159→
   160→```bash
   161→npx -y tessl skill review <staging-skill-dir>
   162→```
   163→
   164→**Parse the output** to extract:
   165→- `description_score` — percentage
   166→- `content_score` — percentage
   167→- `average_score` — percentage
   168→- `validation_result` — PASSED/FAILED
   169→- `judge_suggestions[]` — list of improvement suggestions
   170→
   171→**If tessl content score < 70%:** Record a warning in the evidence report:
   172→
   173→"Content quality warning: tessl scored content at {score}%. This often indicates SKILL.md lacks inline actionable content after split-body. Consider inlining Quick Start and common workflows directly in SKILL.md."
   174→
   175→**If tessl unavailable:** Skip with note: "Content quality review skipped — tessl tool unavailable"
   176→
   177→**Note:** tessl installs automatically via `npx`. A missing tool is not an error — graceful skip.
   178→
   179→### 7. Validate metadata.json
   180→
   181→Cross-check metadata.json content against extraction inventory:
   182→- `stats.exports_documented` matches actual documented exports
   183→- `stats.exports_total` matches total extracted exports
   184→- `stats.coverage` is accurate (documented / total)
   185→- `confidence_t1`, `confidence_t2`, `confidence_t3` match actual counts
   186→- `spec_version` is "1.3"
   187→
   188→Auto-fix any discrepancies (these are computed values).
   189→
   190→### 8. Update Evidence Report
   191→
   192→Add validation results to the evidence-report content in context:
   193→
   194→```markdown
   195→## Validation Results
   196→- Schema: {pass/fail} (quality score: {score}/100)
   197→- Frontmatter: {pass/fail}
   198→- Body: {pass/fail} {split-body applied if applicable}
   199→- Security: {pass/warn/skipped}
   200→- Content Quality (tessl): {pass/warn/skipped} (score: {score}%)
   201→- Metadata: {pass/fail}
   202→
   203→## Quality Score Breakdown
   204→- Frontmatter (30%): {score}
   205→- Description (30%): {score}
   206→- Body (20%): {score}
   207→- Links (10%): {score}
   208→- File (10%): {score}
   209→
   210→## Auto-Fixed Issues
   211→- {list of issues automatically corrected by --fix}
   212→
   213→## Remaining Warnings
   214→- {any warnings from validation}
   215→
   216→## Security Findings
   217→- {any security scan results}
   218→
   219→## Content Quality (tessl)
   220→- {tessl average score, description score, content score, or "skipped"}
   221→- {judge suggestions if available}
   222→```
   223→
   224→### 9. Menu Handling Logic
   225→
   226→**Auto-proceed step — no user interaction.**
   227→
   228→After validation is complete (or skipped if tools unavailable), immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
   229→
   230→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   231→
   232→- This is an auto-proceed validation step with no user choices
   233→- Tool unavailability is a skip, not a halt
   234→- Validation failures are warnings — proceed to artifact generation
   235→- Proceed directly to next step after validation completes
   236→
   237→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   238→
   239→ONLY WHEN validation is complete (or skipped) and evidence-report content is updated will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for artifact generation.
   240→
   241→---
   242→
   243→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   244→
   245→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   246→
   247→- `npx skill-check check --fix --format json` executed (or skipped with warning if unavailable)
   248→- Quality score (0-100) captured and recorded in evidence report
   249→- Auto-fix applied via `--fix` for deterministic issues
   250→- Security scan executed as separate pass (or skipped with warning)
   251→- Split-body applied if `body.max_lines` failed
   252→- `npx -y tessl skill review` executed (or skipped with warning if unavailable)
   253→- Content quality warning raised if tessl content score < 70%
   254→- Metadata cross-check performed
   255→- Evidence report updated with structured validation results (including tessl scores)
   256→- Auto-proceeded to step-07
   257→
   258→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   259→
   260→- Halting the workflow on validation failure (should warn and proceed)
   261→- Halting on skill-check unavailability (should skip with warning)
   262→- Adding new content during validation (only structural fixes allowed)
   263→- Not recording quality score in evidence report
   264→- Skipping security scan without recording the skip
   265→- Attempting more than one auto-fix cycle per failure
   266→
   267→**Master Rule:** Validation informs, it does not block. Record results, fix what's deterministic, scan for security issues, warn about the rest, and proceed.
   268→

     1→# Scoring Rules
     2→
     3→## Default Threshold
     4→
     5→**Pass threshold:** 80%
     6→
     7→## Category Weights
     8→
     9→| Category | Weight | Description |
    10→|----------|--------|-------------|
    11→| Export Coverage | 36% | Percentage of source exports documented in SKILL.md |
    12→| Signature Accuracy | 22% | Documented signatures match actual source signatures |
    13→| Type Coverage | 14% | Types and interfaces referenced are complete |
    14→| Coherence (contextual) | 18% | Cross-references valid, integration patterns complete |
    15→| Coherence (naive) | 0% | Not applicable — weight redistributed to other categories |
    16→| External Validation | 10% | Average of skill-check quality score + tessl average score (redistributed if unavailable) |
    17→
    18→## Naive Mode Weight Redistribution
    19→
    20→When running in naive mode (no coherence category):
    21→- Export Coverage: 45%
    22→- Signature Accuracy: 25%
    23→- Type Coverage: 20%
    24→- External Validation: 10%
    25→
    26→## External Validation Unavailable
    27→
    28→When neither skill-check nor tessl is available, redistribute the 10% external validation weight proportionally to the other active categories. When only one tool is available, use that tool's score as the external validation score.
    29→
    30→## Tier-Dependent Scoring
    31→
    32→### Quick Tier (no tools)
    33→- Export Coverage: file/structure existence check only
    34→- Signature Accuracy: skipped (no AST)
    35→- Type Coverage: skipped (no AST)
    36→- Score based on: structural completeness only
    37→
    38→### Forge Tier (ast-grep)
    39→- Export Coverage: AST-backed export comparison
    40→- Signature Accuracy: AST-verified signature matching
    41→- Type Coverage: AST-verified type completeness
    42→- Full scoring formula applied
    43→
    44→### Deep Tier (ast-grep + gh + QMD)
    45→- All Forge tier checks plus:
    46→- Cross-repository reference verification
    47→- QMD knowledge enrichment for coherence
    48→- Full scoring formula with maximum depth
    49→
    50→## Score Calculation
    51→
    52→```
    53→score = sum(category_weight * category_score) for each category
    54→category_score = (items_passing / items_total) * 100
    55→```
    56→
    57→## Coherence Score Aggregation (Contextual Mode)
    58→
    59→```
    60→reference_validity = (valid_references / total_references) * 100
    61→integration_completeness = (complete_patterns / total_patterns) * 100
    62→combined_coherence = (reference_validity * 0.6) + (integration_completeness * 0.4)
    63→```
    64→
    65→If no integration patterns exist, combined coherence equals reference validity.
    66→
    67→## Result Determination
    68→
    69→- score >= threshold → PASS
    70→- score < threshold → FAIL
    71→
    72→## Gap Severity
    73→
    74→| Severity | Criteria |
    75→|----------|----------|
    76→| Critical | Missing exported function/class documentation |
    77→| High | Signature mismatch between source and SKILL.md |
    78→| Medium | Missing type or interface documentation |
    79→| Low | Missing optional metadata or examples |
    80→| Info | Style suggestions, non-blocking observations |
    81→
```

  "new_string": "For each major exported function — limited to the **top-level public API surface** (typically 10-20 functions that will appear in the context-snippet), not all extracted exports — search the temporal QMD collections for context:\n\n**Search query construction:**\n\nFor each function, derive the **module context** from the extraction inventory's source file path (e.g., `src/graph/neo4j/index.ts` → module context `graph neo4j`). This context improves search relevance by scoping results to the function's subsystem without adding extra queries.\n\n**Search targets per function:**\n\n1. **Issues/PRs:** `qmd_bridge.search(\"{module_context} {function_name}\")` — find related discussions scoped by module context\n2. **Changelog entries:** `qmd_bridge.search(\"{function_name} changelog\")` — find version history, breaking changes (kept generic — changelogs rarely use module paths)\n3. **Migration notes:** `qmd_bridge.vector_search(\"{module_context} {function_name} migration deprecated\")` — find deprecation or migration context scoped by module (semantic search benefits from richer context)"
}

Format as a markdown document with one section per function name, listing the matching issues/PRs found.\n\n**If `gh search` is unavailable** (older `gh` CLI versions): skip targeted searches silently. The generic fetches from section 3 still provide baseline temporal context.\n\n**If rate limiting occurs** (HTTP 429 or similar): stop targeted searches immediately, keep results collected so far. Log: \"Targeted search stopped at function {N}/{total} due to rate limiting.\"\n\n**After all fetching,** verify at least one file was written to the staging directory. If the staging directory is empty (all fetches failed), log a warning and skip to section 5."
}

    93→3. **Migration notes:** `qmd_bridge.vector_search("{module_context} {function_name} migration deprecated")` — find deprecation or migration context scoped by module (semantic search benefits from richer context)
    94→

    96→
    97→- Create a T2 annotation: `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]`
    98→- Classify temporal relevance:
    99→  - **T2-past:** Historical context (why it was built, what it replaced)
   100→  - **T2-future:** Forward-looking context (planned changes, deprecation warnings)
   101→
   102→**Handling QMD failures:**
   103→
   104→- If qmd_bridge is unavailable: skip all enrichment, note in context
   105→- If individual search fails: skip that function's enrichment, continue with others

    43→
    44→### Deep Tier (ast-grep + gh + QMD)
    45→- All Forge tier checks plus:
    46→- Cross-repository reference verification
    47→- QMD knowledge enrichment for coherence
    48→- Full scoring formula with maximum depth
    49→- **Migration & Deprecation Warnings section:** If T2-future annotations exist in the enrichment data, verify that Section 4b is present in SKILL.md Tier 1 and that each warning traces to a T2 provenance citation. If no T2-future annotations exist, Section 4b should be absent (not empty). Presence/absence mismatch is a Medium severity gap.
    50→
    51→## Score Calculation
    52→
    53→```
    54→score = sum(category_weight * category_score) for each category
    55→category_score = (items_passing / items_total) * 100
    56→```
    57→
    58→## Coherence Score Aggregation (Contextual Mode)
    59→
    60→```
    61→reference_validity = (valid_references / total_references) * 100
    62→integration_completeness = (complete_patterns / total_patterns) * 100
    63→combined_coherence = (reference_validity * 0.6) + (integration_completeness * 0.4)
    64→```
    65→
    66→If no integration patterns exist, combined coherence equals reference validity.
    67→
    68→## Result Determination
    69→
    70→- score >= threshold → PASS
    71→- score < threshold → FAIL
    72→
    73→## Gap Severity
    74→
    75→| Severity | Criteria |
    76→|----------|----------|
    77→| Critical | Missing exported function/class documentation |
    78→| High | Signature mismatch between source and SKILL.md |
    79→| Medium | Missing type or interface documentation |
    80→| Medium | Migration section present/absent mismatch with T2-future annotation data (Deep tier) |
    81→| Low | Missing optional metadata or examples |
    82→| Info | Style suggestions, non-blocking observations |

     1→---
     2→name: validate-workflow

     6→---
     7→
     8→# Validate Workflow
     9→
    10→**Goal:** Validate existing workflows against BMAD standards through comprehensive review.
    11→
    12→**Your Role:** Validation Architect and Quality Assurance Specialist. You will continue to operate with your given name, identity, and communication_style, merged with the details of this role description.
    13→
    14→---
    15→
    16→## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
    17→
    18→This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution:
    19→
    20→### Core Principles
    21→
    22→- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is a self contained instruction file that is a part of an overall workflow that must be followed exactly
    23→- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory - never load future step files until told to do so
    24→- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order, no skipping or optimization allowed
    25→- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter using `stepsCompleted` array when a workflow produces a document
    26→- **Append-Only Building**: Build documents by appending content as directed to the output file
    27→
    28→### Step Processing Rules
    29→
    30→1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action
    31→2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute all numbered sections in order, never deviate
    32→3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: If a menu is presented, halt and wait for user selection
    33→4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue as an option, only proceed to next step when user selects 'C' (Continue)
    34→5. **SAVE STATE**: Update `stepsCompleted` in frontmatter before loading next step
    35→6. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, load, read entire file, then execute the next step file
    36→
    37→### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
    38→
    39→- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
    40→- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
    41→- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
    42→- 💾 **ALWAYS** update frontmatter of output files when writing the final output for a specific step
    43→- 🎯 **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
    44→- ⏸️ **ALWAYS** halt at menus and wait for user input
    45→- 📋 **NEVER** create mental todo lists from future steps
    46→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT In your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    47→
    48→---
    49→
    50→## INITIALIZATION SEQUENCE
    51→
    52→### 1. Configuration Loading
    53→
    54→Load and read full config from {project-root}/_bmad/bmb/config.yaml and resolve:
    55→
    56→- `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `document_output_language`, `bmb_creations_output_folder`
    57→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT In your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    58→
    59→### 2. Route to Validate Workflow
    60→
    61→"**Validate Mode: Validating an existing workflow against BMAD standards.**"
    62→
    63→Prompt for workflow path: "Which workflow would you like to validate? Please provide the path to the workflow.md file."
    64→
    65→Then load, read completely, and execute `{validateWorkflow}` (steps-v/step-01-validate.md)

     1→# Step File Rules
     2→
     3→**Purpose:** Quick reference for step structure and compliance. See linked data files for detailed standards.
     4→
     5→---
     6→
     7→## File Size Limits
     8→
     9→| Metric      | Value    |
    10→| ----------- | -------- |
    11→| Recommended | < 200 lines |
    12→| Absolute Maximum | 250 lines |
    13→
    14→**If exceeded:** Split into multiple steps or extract to `/data/` files.
    15→
    16→---
    17→
    18→## Required Step Structure
    19→
    20→```markdown
    21→---
    22→name: 'step-[N]-[name]'
    23→description: '[what this step does]'
    24→
    25→# File References (ONLY variables used in this step!)
    26→[file references in {variable} format
    27→---
    28→
    29→# Step [N]: [Name]
    30→
    31→## STEP GOAL:
    32→[Single sentence: what this step accomplishes]
    33→
    34→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    35→### Universal Rules:
    36→- NEVER generate content without user input
    37→- CRITICAL: Read complete step file before taking action
    38→- CRITICAL: When loading next step with 'C', ensure entire file is read
    39→- YOU ARE A FACILITATOR, not a content generator
    40→
    41→### Role Reinforcement:
    42→- You are a [specific role]
    43→- We engage in collaborative dialogue, not command-response
    44→- You bring [expertise], user brings [theirs]
    45→- Together we produce something better
    46→
    47→### Step-Specific Rules:
    48→- Focus only on [specific task]
    49→- FORBIDDEN to [prohibited action]
    50→- Approach: [how to engage]
    51→
    52→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    53→- [Protocol 1]
    54→- [Protocol 2 - save/update]
    55→- [Protocol 3 - tracking]
    56→
    57→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    58→- Available context: [what's available]
    59→- Focus: [what to focus on]
    60→- Limits: [boundaries]
    61→- Dependencies: [what this depends on]
    62→
    63→## Sequence of Instructions:
    64→### 1. [Action]
    65→[Instructions]
    66→
    67→### N. Present MENU OPTIONS
    68→[Menu section - see menu-handling-standards.md]
    69→
    70→## SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS:
    71→### SUCCESS:
    72→[Success criteria]
    73→### SYSTEM FAILURE:
    74→[Failure criteria]
    75→**Master Rule:** Skipping steps is FORBIDDEN.
    76→```
    77→
    78→---
    79→
    80→## Critical Rules (Quick Reference)
    81→
    82→### Frontmatter
    83→- Only variables USED in step body
    84→- All file references use `{variable}` format
    85→- Relative paths within workflow folder
    86→- See: `frontmatter-standards.md`
    87→
    88→### Menus
    89→- Handler section MUST follow display
    90→- "Halt and wait" in execution rules
    91→- A/P options only when appropriate
    92→- Non-C options redisplay menu
    93→- See: `menu-handling-standards.md`
    94→
    95→### Progressive Disclosure
    96→- Only load next step when user selects 'C'
    97→- Read entire step file before execution
    98→- Don't create mental todos from future steps
    99→
   100→### Continuable Workflows
   101→- Append step number to `stepsCompleted`
   102→- Don't hardcode full array
   103→- See: `workflow-type-criteria.md`
   104→
   105→---
   106→
   107→## Data Files Reference
   108→
   109→| File | Purpose |
   110→| --- | --- |
   111→| `frontmatter-standards.md` | Variables, paths, frontmatter rules |
   112→| `menu-handling-standards.md` | Menu patterns, handler requirements |

   121→
   122→| Step Type | Template/Reference |

   154→
   155→---
   156→
   157→## Validation Checklist
   158→
   159→For every step file:
   160→
   161→- [ ] File < 200 lines (250 max)
   162→- [ ] `name` and `description` in frontmatter
   163→- [ ] All frontmatter variables are used
   164→- [ ] File references use `{variable}` format
   165→- [ ] Relative paths within workflow folder
   166→- [ ] Handler section follows menu display
   167→- [ ] "Halt and wait" in execution rules
   168→- [ ] A/P options appropriate for step type
   169→- [ ] C option saves and loads next step
   170→- [ ] Non-C options redisplay menu
   171→- [ ] StepsCompleted appended (if continuable)
   172→- [ ] Success/failure metrics present
   173→
   174→---
   175→
   176→## Quick Menu Reference
   177→
   178→```markdown
   179→### N. Present MENU OPTIONS
   180→
   181→Display: "**Select:** [A] [action A] [P] [action P] [C] Continue"
   182→
   183→#### Menu Handling Logic:
   184→- IF A: Execute {advancedElicitationTask}, and when finished redisplay the menu
   185→- IF P: Execute {partyModeWorkflow}, and when finished redisplay the menu
   186→- IF C: Save content to {outputFile}, update frontmatter, then load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
   187→- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond then [Redisplay Menu Options](#n-present-menu-options)
   188→
   189→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   190→- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
   191→- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
   192→- After other menu items execution, return to this menu
   193→```
   194→
   195→---
   196→
   197→## Common Violations
   198→
   199→| Violation | Fix |
   200→| --- | --- |
   201→| Unused variable in frontmatter | Remove unused variables |
   202→| Hardcoded file path | Use `{variable}` format |
   203→| A/P menu in step 1 | Remove A/P (inappropriate for init) |
   204→| Missing handler section | Add handler after menu display |
   205→| No "halt and wait" instruction | Add to EXECUTION RULES |
   206→| Hardcoded `stepsCompleted: [1,2,3]` | Append: "update stepsCompleted to add this step" |
   207→| File > 250 lines | Split into multiple steps or extract to /data/ |
   208→| Absolute path for same-folder ref | Use relative path or `{workflow_path}` |
   209→
   210→---
   211→
   212→## When to Extract to Data Files
   213→
   214→Extract step content to `/data/` when:
   215→- Step file exceeds 200 lines
   216→- Content is reference material
   217→- Content is reused across steps
   218→- Content is domain-specific (examples, patterns)
   219→
   220→**Data file types:**
   221→- `.md` - Reference documentation
   222→- `.csv` - Structured data for lookup
   223→- `examples/` - Reference implementations
   224→
   225→---
   226→
   227→## Tri-Modal Workflow Note
   228→
   229→For Create/Edit/Validate workflows:
   230→- Each mode has its own `steps-c/`, `steps-e/`, `steps-v/` folder
   231→- NO shared step files (`s-*.md`) between modes
   232→- All modes share `/data/` folder
   233→- This prevents confusion and routing errors
   234→
   235→See: `trimodal-workflow-structure.md`
   236→
```

    31→- 💬 Subprocess must either update validation report directly OR return structured findings to parent for aggregation
    32→- 🚪 This is validation - systematic and thorough using per-file deep analysis (Pattern 2)

    35→

    37→- 💾 Subprocesses must either update validation report OR return findings for parent aggregation
    38→- 📖 Aggregate all findings into validation report before loading next step

    41→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:

    43→- All step files in the workflow must be validated

    46→
    47→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    48→
    49→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut.
    50→
    51→### 1. Load Frontmatter Standards
    52→
    53→Load {frontmatterStandards} to understand validation criteria.
    54→
    55→**Key Rules:**
    56→1. Only variables USED in the step may be in frontmatter
    57→2. All file references MUST use `{variable}` format
    58→3. Paths within workflow folder MUST be relative - NO `workflow_path` allowed
    59→
    60→**Forbidden Patterns:**
    61→- `workflow_path: '...'` - use relative paths instead
    62→- `thisStepFile: '...'` - remove unless actually referenced in body
    63→- `workflowFile: '...'` - remove unless actually referenced in body
    64→- `./...` - use `./step-XX.md`
    65→- `{workflow_path}/templates/...` - use `../template.md`
    66→
    67→### 2. Validate EVERY Step File - Systematic Algorithm with Subprocess Optimization
    68→
    69→**DO NOT BE LAZY - For EACH step file, launch a subprocess that:**
    70→
    71→1. Loads that file
    72→2. Loads {frontmatterStandards} to understand validation criteria
    73→3. Performs all frontmatter validation checks on that file (extract variables, check usage, validate paths)
    74→4. **EITHER** updates the validation report directly with its findings
    75→5. **OR** returns structured findings to parent for aggregation
    76→
    77→**SUBPROCESS ANALYSIS PATTERN:**
    78→
    79→For each file, the subprocess performs the following deep analysis:
    80→
    81→#### Step 2.1: Extract Frontmatter Variables
    82→
    83→```python
    84→# Algorithm to extract variables from frontmatter:
    85→1. Find content between first `---` and second `---`
    86→2. For each line, extract key before `:`
    87→3. Skip `name`, `description`, and comment lines starting with `#`
    88→4. Collect all variable names
    89→```
    90→
    91→Example frontmatter:
    92→```yaml
    93→---
    94→# File References
    95→nextStepFile: './step-02-vision.md'
    96→outputFile: '{planning_artifacts}/product-brief-{{project_name}}.md'
    97→workflow_path: '{project-root}/...'  # ❌ FORBIDDEN
    98→thisStepFile: './step-01-init.md'     # ❌ Likely unused
    99→---
   100→```
   101→
   102→Variables extracted: `nextStepFile`, `outputFile`, `workflow_path`, `thisStepFile`
   103→
   104→#### Step 2.2: Check Each Variable Is Used
   105→
   106→```python
   107→# Algorithm to check variable usage:
   108→for each variable in extracted_variables:
   109→    search_body = "{variableName}"  # with curly braces
   110→    if search_body NOT found in step body (after frontmatter):
   111→        MARK_AS_UNUSED(variable)
   112→```
   113→
   114→**Example:**
   115→- Variable `nextStepFile`: Search body for `{nextStepFile}` → Found in line 166 ✅
   116→- Variable `thisStepFile`: Search body for `{thisStepFile}` → Not found ❌ VIOLATION
   117→
   118→#### Step 2.3: Check Path Formats
   119→
   120→For each variable containing a file path:
   121→
   122→```python
   123→# Algorithm to validate paths:
   124→if path contains "{workflow_path}":
   125→    MARK_AS_VIOLATION("workflow_path is forbidden - use relative paths")
   126→
   127→if path is to another step file:
   128→    if not path.startswith("./step-"):
   129→        MARK_AS_VIOLATION("Step-to-step paths must be ./filename.md")
   130→
   131→if path is to parent folder template:
   132→    if not path.startswith("../"):
   133→        MARK_AS_VIOLATION("Parent folder paths must be ../filename.md")
   134→
   135→if path contains "{project-root}" and is internal workflow reference:
   136→    MARK_AS_VIOLATION("Internal paths must be relative, not project-root")
   137→```
   138→
   139→**RETURN FORMAT:**
   140→
   141→Subprocess returns file name, frontmatter compliance status, unused variables found, path violations, and overall status (PASS/FAIL). Include specific variable names and violation details for documentation.
   142→
   143→Check ALL files systematically. Return findings for compilation and appendage to validation report.
   144→
   145→### 3. Aggregate Findings and Document Results
   146→
   147→Document frontmatter validation results in the validation report showing:
   148→- Which files were checked
   149→- Frontmatter compliance status for each file
   150→- Unused variables found in each file
   151→- Path violations detected
   152→- Overall pass/fail status for each file

   154→### 4. List All Violations

   161→- Files that passed all checks

   163→### 5. Append to Report

   166→

## Workflow Specification Validation Report

**Scope:** `src/workflows/test-skill/data/scoring-rules.md` (edited file) and consuming step files: step-04-coherence-check.md, step-04b-external-validators.md, step-05-score.md.

## Check A: New Deep Tier Scoring Rule

**Question:** Does step-05-score.md consume scoring-rules.md? Does it reference tier-dependent scoring? Would the new rule be picked up?

**Findings:**

step-05-score.md frontmatter at line 7 declares `scoringRulesFile: '../data/scoring-rules.md'`. Its Mandatory Sequence Section 1 explicitly instructs: "Load `{scoringRulesFile}` to get: Category weights, Default threshold (80%), **Tier-dependent scoring adjustments**."

The phrase "Tier-dependent scoring adjustments" is exactly the heading under which the new Deep Tier rule lives in scoring-rules.md. However, what step-05 does with those tier-dependent rules is narrow: it only uses them to determine whether `Signature Accuracy` and `Type Coverage` are N/A for Quick tier (Section 3, "Quick tier adjustment"). The new Deep tier bullet describes a **content verification check** (does Section 4b exist? does each warning trace to a T2 provenance citation?), not a weight adjustment.

**Problem:** No step in the test-skill workflow is assigned responsibility for executing the Section 4b presence/absence verification described by the new rule. Step-05 loads scoring-rules.md for weight distribution and threshold values only. It does not iterate over tier-dependent bullets to perform content checks — that role belongs to step-04 (coherence-check). Step-04 has no instruction to read scoring-rules.md at all, and its context boundary explicitly limits it to "Internal consistency and reference validation." Step-03 (coverage-check) reads scoring-rules.md but only for category score formulas.

The new rule is written as a **check to perform**, not as a scoring formula or weight. As written, it will be loaded by step-05 but no step has the mandate to act on it. The rule describes behavior that belongs in step-04-coherence-check.md's Deep tier section, not in scoring-rules.md's tier-dependent scoring block.

**RESULT: FAIL**

The new rule is stranded. It is syntactically present in scoring-rules.md but no consuming step has instructions to execute the described check. Step-05 loads the file but treats tier-dependent scoring as weight/tier adjustments, not content verification checks. Step-04 does not read scoring-rules.md. Neither step-04 nor step-05 would perform the Section 4b presence/absence verification in practice.

**Required fix:** Either (a) add a Deep-tier sub-section to step-04-coherence-check.md instructing it to read scoring-rules.md's tier-dependent rules and execute the Section 4b verification, or (b) move the rule into step-04-coherence-check.md directly as a Deep-tier contextual check, and leave scoring-rules.md for scoring mechanics only. Option (b) is cleaner because step-04 already has a "CONTEXT BOUNDARIES" section listing available artifacts.

## Check B: New Gap Severity Table Entry

**Question:** Is the new Medium row consistent with the table format? Do step-04 or step-05 reference gap severities?

**Table format check:**

Existing rows in scoring-rules.md:

```
| Severity | Criteria |
|----------|----------|
| Critical | Missing exported function/class documentation |
| High     | Signature mismatch between source and SKILL.md |
| Medium   | Missing type or interface documentation |
| Medium   | Migration section present/absent mismatch with T2-future annotation data (Deep tier) |
| Low      | Missing optional metadata or examples |
| Info     | Style suggestions, non-blocking observations |
```

The new row uses the same two-column `| Severity | Criteria |` format. The value "Medium" is consistent with the existing severity vocabulary (Critical/High/Medium/Low/Info). The parenthetical "(Deep tier)" is stylistically consistent with the document's convention of annotating tier-scoped rules inline. No format inconsistency.

**Consumer check:**

step-06-report.md is the sole consumer of gap severity classifications. Its Section 2 ("Load Severity Rules") loads `{scoringRulesFile}` and reproduces the full severity table inline in the step file. Critically, the inline table in step-06-report.md (lines 76-82 of that file) only lists the original five severity rows — it does not yet include the new Medium row for Migration section mismatch.

**Problem:** step-06-report.md hard-codes a copy of the severity table inline at Section 2. The step then says: "assign severity from `{scoringRulesFile}`" — meaning it defers to the loaded file at classification time. The inline copy is illustrative, not normative. An executing LLM reading step-06 would see the inline table (without the new row) first, then load scoring-rules.md (with the new row). Whether the LLM correctly reconciles the two depends on which it treats as authoritative.

This is a latent inconsistency rather than a hard failure: the instruction says "from `{scoringRulesFile}`" so the loaded file takes precedence. But the stale inline copy in step-06 creates a documentation drift risk.

**RESULT: PASS with Advisory**

The format is correct and the entry is consistent. The consuming step (step-06) defers to scoring-rules.md by reference, so the new entry will be picked up at classification time. However, the inline severity table reproduced in step-06-report.md lines 76-82 is now stale and should be updated to include the new Medium row, or removed in favor of a directive to load-and-apply from scoring-rules.md only.

Relevant file for the advisory: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md` lines 74-82.
